Appendix B
Useful Functions and Properties

In this appendix, we collect a few special functions and their properties used in the main text. These are standard material, and we quote them from
[
170
,
171
]
.

Euler
\mathbf{Gamma}
products and fractions

In this work we use the following shorthand notation for the productions and fractions of Euler
\Gamma
functions:

\Gamma\big[a_{1},\cdots,a_{n}\big]\equiv\Gamma(a_{1})\cdots\Gamma(a_{n});

(150)

\displaystyle\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{m}\\
b_{1},b_{2},\cdots,b_{n}\end{matrix}\biggr]\equiv\displaystyle\frac{\,{\Gamma(a_{1})\Gamma(a_{2})\cdots\Gamma(a_{m})}\,}{\Gamma(b_{1})\Gamma(b_{2})\cdots\Gamma(b_{n})}.

(151)

With this notation, the Pochhammer symbol
(a)_{n}
is defined as

\displaystyle(a)_{n}\equiv\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}a+n\\
a\end{matrix}\biggr].

(152)

Hypergeometric functions

The (generalized) hypergeometric function is used in this work, whose standard form is defined by the following series when convergent, and by analytical continuation otherwise:

{}_{p}\mathrm{F}_{q}\biggl[\begin{matrix}a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{p}\\
b_{1},b_{2},\cdots,b_{q}\end{matrix}\bigg|z\biggr]\equiv\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(a_{1})_{n}(a_{2})_{n}\cdots(a_{p})_{n}}\,}{(b_{1})_{n}(b_{2})_{n}\cdots(b_{q})_{n}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{z^{n}}\,}{n!}.

(153)

In particular,
{}_{2}\text{F}_{1}
is known as the Gauss’s or ordinary hypergeometric function.

There are a few useful variations whose definitions are different from the standard form only in prefactors. First, the
regularized
hypergeometric function
{}_{p}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathrm{F}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{q}
is defined by:

\displaystyle{}_{p}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathrm{F}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{q}\biggl[\begin{matrix}a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{p}\\
b_{1},b_{2},\cdots,b_{q}\end{matrix}\bigg|z\biggr]\equiv\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{\Gamma[b_{1},b_{2},\cdots,b_{q}]}{}_{p}\mathrm{F}_{q}\biggl[\begin{matrix}a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{p}\\
b_{1},b_{2},\cdots,b_{q}\end{matrix}\bigg|z\biggr].

(154)

It is called regularized, because, when the argument
z
is not at the singular points, the regularized hypergeometric function is an entire function of all the parameters
(a_{1},\cdots,a_{p},b_{1},\cdots,b_{q})
.

Second, we frequently use the “dressed” hypergeometric function
{}_{p}{\mathcal{F}}_{q}
in the main text, because it simplifies a lot of expressions:

\displaystyle{}_{p}{\mathcal{F}}_{q}\biggl[\begin{matrix}a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{p}\\
b_{1},b_{2},\cdots,b_{q}\end{matrix}\bigg|z\biggr]\equiv\displaystyle\frac{\,{\Gamma[a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{p}]}\,}{\Gamma[b_{1},b_{2},\cdots,b_{q}]}{}_{p}\mathrm{F}_{q}\biggl[\begin{matrix}a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{p}\\
b_{1},b_{2},\cdots,b_{q}\end{matrix}\bigg|z\biggr].

(155)

It is useful to note that the Gauss’s hypergeometric function
{}_{2}\text{F}_{1}[\cdots|z]
in general has two branch points at
z=1
and
z=\infty
. It is our convention to choose the branch cut connecting these two points to lie in the interval
z\in(1,\infty)
on the real axis. We define the value of
{}_{2}\text{F}_{1}[\cdots|z]
when
z>1
by its value on the
lower
edge of the branch cut. Then, the value on the upper edge is determined by the discontinuity across the branch cut. More explicitly:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &{}_{2}\mathrm{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}a,b\\
c\end{matrix}\bigg|z^{+}\biggr]\\
=&\ 2\pi\mathrm{i}e^{\pi\mathrm{i}(a+b-c)}\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}c\\
a+b-c+1,c-a,c-b\end{matrix}\biggr]{}_{2}\mathrm{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}a,b\\
a+b-c+1\end{matrix}\bigg|1-z\biggr]\\
&+e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}(a+b-c)}{}_{2}\mathrm{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}a,b\\
c\end{matrix}\bigg|z\biggr],\\
&{}_{2}\mathrm{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}a,b\\
c\end{matrix}\bigg|z^{-}\biggr]={}_{2}\mathrm{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}a,b\\
c\end{matrix}\bigg|z\biggr].\end{aligned}\right.

(156)

For power functions with non-integer powers, we can get a branch cut along the negative real axis by restricting the argument of variable in
(-\pi,\pi]
.

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &(ze^{\mathrm{i}\pi})^{p}=e^{\mathrm{i}\pi p}z^{p},\\
&(ze^{-\mathrm{i}\pi})^{p}=e^{-\mathrm{i}\pi p}z^{p}.~~~~(z>0)\end{aligned}

(157)

Bessel functions

In this work, we used the standard Bessel J function, especially its analytical property. Generically, a Bessel J function
J_{\nu}(z)
has a branch cut on the negative real axis, connecting
z=0
and
z=-\infty
. The discontinuity across this branch cut is conveniently captured by the following identity:

\displaystyle\mathrm{J}_{\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(e^{m\pi\mathrm{i}}z)=e^{\mp m\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\pi}\mathrm{J}_{\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(z).~~~~(z>0)

(158)

More frequently appeared in the main text are Hankel functions
\mathrm{H}_{\nu}^{(1)}
and
\mathrm{H}_{\nu}^{(2)}
, which can be expressed in terms of Bessel J function as:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathrm{H}^{(1)}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(z)=\bigl(1+\coth(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\bigr)\mathrm{J}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(z)-\mathrm{csch}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\mathrm{J}_{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(z),\\
&\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(z)=-\mathrm{csch}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\mathrm{J}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(z)+\bigl(1+\coth(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\bigr)\mathrm{J}_{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(z).\end{aligned}

(159)

Consequently, the Hankel functions
\mathrm{H}_{\nu}^{(j)}(z)

(j=1,2)
possess branch cuts on the negative real axis of
z
, whose discontinuity can be found from the following identities:

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{(1)}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(ze^{\mathrm{i}\pi})=-e^{\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(z),

(160)

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{(1)}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(ze^{-\mathrm{i}\pi})=2\cosh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\mathrm{H}^{(1)}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(z)+e^{\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(z),

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(ze^{\mathrm{i}\pi})=e^{\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathrm{H}^{(1)}_{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(z)+2\cosh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(z),

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(ze^{-\mathrm{i}\pi})=-e^{\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathrm{H}^{(1)}_{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(z).~~~~(z>0)

Appendix C
Vertex Dispersion Integral with PMB Representation

In this section we collect some details of computing the 4-point single-exchange correlator from the vertex dispersion integral (
3.3
). As shown in Sec.
3.3
, the vertex dispersion integral for the 4-point tree seed integral can be reduced to (
3.3
), which in turns amount to the computation of two integrals
\mathbf{I}^{(j)}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}
(
j=1,2
), which we collect here again:

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}(r_{1},r_{2})\equiv\int_{-r_{2}}^{0}{\mathrm{d}}r\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-r)^{1/2-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(-r)}\,}{r(r-r_{1})},

(161)

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)}(r_{1},r_{2})\equiv\int_{-1}^{-r_{2}}{\mathrm{d}}r\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-r)^{1/2-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(-r)}\,}{r(r-r_{1})}.

(162)

Computing
\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}
and
\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)}

Now we compute the two integrals above with PMB representation. For
\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}
, we take the MB representation of
\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(-r)
, which is given by:

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(-r)=\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}(-r)^{-s}\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s),

(163)

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s)\equiv-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi 2^{-1+s+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}e^{-\mathrm{i}\pi s/2}}\,}{\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{s}\,}{2}$},\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-s-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1-\mbox{$\frac{\,{s}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr].

(164)

then the original integral
\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}
becomes:

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}=\int_{-r_{2}}^{0}{\mathrm{d}}r\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-r)^{1/2-s-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{r(r-r_{1})}\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s).

(165)

The integral over
r
can then be finished directly, which gives:

\displaystyle\int_{-r_{2}}^{0}{\mathrm{d}}r\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-r)^{1/2-s-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{r(r-r_{1})}=\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{2}^{1/2-s-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{r_{1}}{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-s-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,1\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-s-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{2}}\,}{r_{1}}\biggr].

(166)

Now, the original integral
\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}
has been recasted into an integral over a Mellin variable
s
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}=\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{2}^{1/2-s-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{r_{1}}{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-s-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,1\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-s-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{2}}\,}{r_{1}}\biggr]\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s).

(167)

Again, we use residue theorem to compute the integral over
s
. For
r_{2}\in(0,1)
, we need to close the contour from the left side, and get a set of poles coming from
\Gamma[s/2]
in
\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s)
:

\displaystyle s=-2n.~~~~(n=0,1,2,\cdots)

(168)

Summing up all residues we get:
9
9

9

Here we introduce the notation
\mathrm{Res}(\mathbf{I},s)
to represent the residue of integral
\mathbf{I}
of the pole at
s
, multiplying an extra factor
2\pi\mathrm{i}
for simplicity. For example, if for integral
\mathbf{I}
there is only one pole
s
inside the contour, then the final result is simply
\mathbf{I}=\pm\mathrm{Res}(\mathbf{I},s)
, where the plus/minus sign depends on the direction of the contour.

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathrm{Res}(\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)},-2n),

(169)

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}(\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)},-2n)=-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi 2^{-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}r_{2}^{{1}/{2}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)r_{1}}\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+n,1+n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr]{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{2}}\,}{r_{1}}\biggr].

(170)

This completes the computation of
\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}
.

Next we consider
\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)}
in (
162
). Again we use the MB representation of
\mathbf{F}^{-2}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}
(
164
) and get:

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)}=\int_{-1}^{-r_{2}}{\mathrm{d}}r\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-r)^{1/2-s-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{r(r-r_{1})}\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s).

(171)

So the integral over
r
can be done:

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{-r_{2}}{\mathrm{d}}r\ \displaystyle\frac{\,{(-r)^{\frac{1}{2}-s-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{r(r-r_{1})}=\Gamma\Bigl[\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+s+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Bigr]

\displaystyle\times\biggl({r_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}-s-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}{}_{2}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathrm{F}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,\frac{1}{2}+s+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\frac{3}{2}+s+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\biggr]-{}_{2}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathrm{F}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,\frac{1}{2}+s+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\frac{3}{2}+s+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-r_{1}\biggr]\biggr),

(172)

where
{}_{2}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathrm{F}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{1}[\cdots]
is the regularized Gauss’s hypergeometric function whose definition is collected in App.
B
.
Again the integral over
s
can be finished via residue theorem. Closing the contour from the left side, there are two sets of poles: one from
\Gamma[s/2]
in
\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s)
, another from
\Gamma[1/2+s+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu]
contributed by the integral over
r
(
C
):

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &s=-2n,\\
&s=-\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu.~~~~(n=0,1,2,\cdots)\end{aligned}\right.

(173)

Then we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathrm{Res}(\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)},-2n)+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathrm{Res}(\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)},-\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu),

(174)

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}(\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)},-2n)=-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi 2^{-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+n,1+n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr]

\displaystyle\times\biggl(r_{2}^{-1/2+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\biggr]-{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-r_{1}\biggr]\biggr),

(175)

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}(\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)},-\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)=

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\frac{\,{\pi 2^{-3/2-n}e^{\mathrm{i}\pi(-1-2n+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)/4}}\,}{\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{n}\,}{2}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{n}\,}{2}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\end{matrix}\biggr]

\displaystyle\times\biggl(r_{2}^{n}{}_{2}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathrm{F}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,-n\\
1-n\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\biggr]-{}_{2}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathrm{F}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,-n\\
1-n\end{matrix}\bigg|-r_{1}\biggr]\biggr).

(176)

Note that

\displaystyle{}_{2}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathrm{F}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,-n\\
1-n\end{matrix}\bigg|x\biggr]=\Gamma[n](-x)^{n},~~~~(n=0,1,2,\cdots)

(177)

so residues from the second set of poles actually vanish:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}(\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)},-\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)=0.

(178)

This completes the computation of
\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)}
. Let us collect the explicit results for both integrals
\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(j)}

(j=1,2)
here for future reference:

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi 2^{-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}r_{2}^{{1}/{2}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)r_{1}}\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+n,1+n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr]{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{2}}\,}{r_{1}}\biggr],

(179)

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{n=0}^{\infty}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi 2^{-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+n,1+n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr]

\displaystyle\times\biggl(r_{2}^{-1/2+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\biggr]-{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-r_{1}\biggr]\biggr).

(180)

Simplifying the result

In principle, we can just substitute the above results for
\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(j)}

(j=1,2)
into (
3.3
) to get an analytical expression for the tree seed integral, but this expression is obviously to be simplified. Now we describe how to massage this expression, using various functional identities, to get a reasonably simplified result.

For definiteness, we consider the case
0<r_{1}<r_{2}<1
without loss of generality.
Given this relation, the result can be separated into the signal and the background without introducing
\theta
factors.

Then, for
\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}
shown in (
179
), we use the following relation:

\displaystyle{}_{2}\mathrm{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}a,b\\
b+1\end{matrix}\bigg|-x\biggr]=x^{-b}\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}a-b,1+b\\
a\end{matrix}\biggr]-\displaystyle\frac{\,{b\times x^{-a}}\,}{a-b}{}_{2}\mathrm{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}a,a-b\\
1+a-b\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{x}\biggr],

(181)

through which we get

\displaystyle{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{2}}\,}{r_{1}}\biggr]=\pi~\mathrm{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Bigl(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\Bigr)^{1/2+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\biggr].

(182)

Then

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle-\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi 2^{-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+n,1+n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr]

\displaystyle\times\biggl(\pi~\mathrm{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu){r_{1}}^{-1/2+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}-{r_{2}}^{-1/2+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\biggr]\biggr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})-\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2}),

(183)

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi^{2}2^{-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{r_{1}}^{-1/2+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\cosh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+n,1+n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr],

(184)

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi 2^{-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{r_{2}}^{-1/2+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+n,1+n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr]{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\biggr].

(185)

Also, if we define

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})\equiv\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi 2^{-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+n,1+n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr]{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-r_{1}\biggr],

(186)

then the second integral
\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)}
can be expressed as:

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)}(r_{1},r_{2})=\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})-\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2}).

(187)

Now, substituting (
C
) and (
187
) into (
3.3
), we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle\ \biggl[\Bigl(\displaystyle\frac{\,{1-\mathrm{i}\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{2\pi^{2}}\cosh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu){r_{1}}\Bigl[\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})-\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})\Bigr]+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Bigr)r_{2}^{1/2-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(r_{2})

\displaystyle+\Bigl({r_{1}}\Bigl[\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})-\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})\Bigr]+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Bigr)\displaystyle\frac{\,{1-\mathrm{i}\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{2\pi^{2}}\cosh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)r_{2}^{1/2-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(r_{2})\biggr]

\displaystyle+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu).

(188)

Signal

One can show that the terms associated with
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{\pm\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}
give the signal part. In fact, the summation in (
184
) can be done:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi^{2}2^{-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{r_{1}}^{-1/2+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\cosh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+n,1+n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\pi~\mathrm{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)r_{1}^{-1/2-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(r_{1}),

(189)

As a result, the terms involving (
184
) in (
C
) gives:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{\mathrm{S},>}(r_{1},r_{2})=\Bigl(\displaystyle\frac{\,{1-\mathrm{i}\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{2\pi}r_{1}^{1/2-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(r_{1})+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Bigr)\Bigl(r_{2}^{1/2-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(r_{2})+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Bigr).

(190)

This is exactly the signal part of the 4-point tree seed integral.

Background

It follows that all other terms besides the
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}
terms give rise to the background:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}

{}_{\mathrm{BG},>}(r_{1},r_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \biggl[\Bigl(\displaystyle\frac{\,{1-\mathrm{i}\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{2\pi^{2}}\cosh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu){r_{1}}\Bigl[-\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})\Bigr]+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Bigr)r_{2}^{1/2-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(r_{2})

\displaystyle+\Bigl({r_{1}}\Bigl[\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})-\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})\Bigr]+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Bigr)\displaystyle\frac{\,{1-\mathrm{i}\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{2\pi^{2}}\cosh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)r_{2}^{1/2-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(r_{2})\biggr]

\displaystyle+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu).

(191)

This expression can be further simplified thanks to several cancellations. First, it is easy to see that all terms including
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}^{\pm\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})\mathbf{F}^{-2}_{\pm\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{2})
cancel out. Second, it can be shown that all terms involving
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{\pm\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})
cancel out. As a result, the background of 4-point seed integral can be simplified into:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{\mathrm{BG},>}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}}\,}{\pi^{2}}\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\cosh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\times{r_{1}}\times r_{2}^{1/2+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})\mathbf{F}^{-2}_{-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{2})+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\coth(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{\pi^{1/2}2^{1/2+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\times r_{1}\times r_{2}^{2n}\times\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+n,1+n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr]

\displaystyle\times{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\biggr]{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\\
1+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|r_{2}^{2}\biggr]\biggr)+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu).

(192)

This is still not the expression found in previous works, but we have checked numerically that it agrees with known results, as mentioned in the main text. There are a large number of functional identities and resummation tricks with which one may prove the agreement analytically, but we shall not pursue this pure mathematical exercise in this work.

Instead, in the rest of this appendix, we prove the cancellation of
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}
terms. More precisely, we shall prove
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})+\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})=0
.

To this end, we use the standard series representation for the dressed hypergeometric function in
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}
:

\displaystyle{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}a,b\\
c\end{matrix}\bigg|x\biggr]=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}a+m,b+m\\
c+m,1+m\end{matrix}\biggr]x^{m}.

(193)

Then, the expression (
186
) for
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}
can be rewritten as:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi 2^{-2n+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+n,1+n-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr]\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-r_{1})^{m}}\,}{\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-2n+m+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}.

(194)

In this expression, the sum over
n
can be directly finished:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi 2^{1+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(-r_{1})^{m}}\,}{(1+2m+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}{}_{3}\mathrm{F}_{2}\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{4}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},-\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{m}\,}{2}$}-\frac{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{2}\\
\frac{3}{4}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{m}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},1-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|1\biggr].

(195)

Then, we use the following identity of
{}_{3}\mathrm{F}_{2}
(Eq. (4.3.4) of
[
172
]
):

\displaystyle{}_{3}\mathrm{F}_{2}\biggl[\begin{matrix}a,b,c\\
d,e\end{matrix}\bigg|1\biggr]=

\displaystyle\ \Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}1-a,d,e,c-b\\
e-b,d-b,1+b-a,c\end{matrix}\biggr]{}_{3}\mathrm{F}_{2}\biggl[\begin{matrix}b,1+b-d,1+b-e\\
1+b-a,1+b-c\end{matrix}\bigg|1\biggr]

\displaystyle+\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}1-a,d,e,b-c\\
e-c,d-c,1+c-a,b\end{matrix}\biggr]{}_{3}\mathrm{F}_{2}\biggl[\begin{matrix}c,1+c-d,1+c-e\\
1+c-a,1+c-b\end{matrix}\bigg|1\biggr].

(196)

Then
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}
can be rewritten as:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}(-r_{1})^{m}}\,}{\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\bigg\{2^{-7/2-m}\Bigl((-1)^{m+1}-1\Bigr)\Gamma\Bigl[1+m,-\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{m}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},-\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{m}\,}{2}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\Bigr]

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\frac{\,{2^{3/2}\pi^{2}}\,}{(2+m)\cosh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Gamma\bigl[\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\bigr]}{}_{3}\mathrm{F}_{2}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1+\mbox{$\frac{\,{m}\,}{2}$},\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{4}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$},2+\mbox{$\frac{\,{m}\,}{2}$}\end{matrix}\bigg|1\biggr]\bigg\}.

(197)

From this expression, it is easy to see
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})=-\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})
.

Appendix D
Line Dispersion Integral with PMB Representation

In this appendix, we spell out the details of computing the three integrals (
141
)-(
143
) arising from the line dispersion relation for the 4-point seed integral (
130
). The strategy is again the PMB representation.

Computing
\mathbf{J}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}

For the first integral
\mathbf{J}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}
, we take the MB representation for
\mathbf{D}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}
appeared in the integrand, whose expression is given in (
5
).
10
10

10

A fine point is that the arguments of the two hypergeometric factors in (
5
) are taken values from the lower edge of the branch cut, where our MB representation for
\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}
in (
164
) is valid. On the contrary, if we want to evaluate the
\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}
on the upper edge of the branch cut, such as
\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(z^{+})
when
z>1
, we need to begin with
\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(z^{-})
, and add back the discontinuity across the branch cut using (
156
).
Then we get:

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}(r_{1},x)={-2\sinh(2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\mathrm{d}}r\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-r)^{1-s_{1}-s_{2}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}x^{s_{2}}}\,}{r(r-r_{1})}\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s_{1})\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s_{2}),

(198)

where
\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s)
is given in (
164
). Then, the
r
integral is again directly done, giving:

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}(r_{1},x)=-2\pi\sinh(2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}^{-s_{1}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1}/x)^{-s_{2}}}\,}{\sin\bigl(\pi(s_{1}+s_{2}+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\bigr)}\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s_{1})\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s_{2}).

(199)

When applying the residue theorem to compute this integral, we meet a subtlety here due to the “mixed poles” such as those from
1/\sin[\pi(s_{1}+s_{2}+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)]
, and we need to deal with these poles carefully. Below we spell out some details.

First, consider the
s_{1}
-integral. Since we want to obtain a result for physical
r_{1}\in(0,1)
, the factor
r_{1}^{-s_{1}}
says that we should close the contour from the left side on the
s_{1}
plane. There are two sets of left poles in
s_{1}
, respectively from
\Gamma(s_{1}/2)
and
1/\sin[\pi(s_{1}+s_{2}+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)]
:

\begin{cases}s_{1}=-2n_{1},\\
s_{1}=-s_{2}-n_{1}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu.~~~~(n_{1}=0,1,2,\cdots)\end{cases}

(200)

After evaluating the Mellin integrand in (
199
) on these two sets of poles respectively, we are left with an
s_{2}
integral. The analysis of the
s_{2}
integral depends on which sets of
s_{1}
-poles we take.

Suppose we take the first set of poles
s_{1}=-2n_{1}
. Then, we are left with a factor of
(r_{1}/x)^{-s_{2}}=r_{2}^{-s_{2}}
. For physical
r_{2}\in(0,1)
, we should pick up left
s_{2}
-poles of the integrand. Examining the integrand in (
199
), we see that there are two sets of left poles:
11
11

11

One may naively think that the second left poles
s_{2}=2n_{1}-n_{2}+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu
with fixed
n_{1}
and
n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
are not all “left,” in the sense that some of these poles have positive real part when
2n_{1}-n_{2}>0
. However, we emphasize that the criterion for a pole being left or right is not the sign of its real part, but rather the sign in front of the natural number
n_{2}
that parameterize the set of poles. Therefore, in this case, all poles with a
-n_{2}
term should be counted as left poles.

\begin{cases}s_{2}=-2n_{2},\\
s_{2}=2n_{1}-n_{2}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu.~~~~(n_{2}=0,1,2,\cdots)\end{cases}

(201)

However, some of “poles” from the second set (e.g., the second line in (
201
)) coincide with zeros from the factor
1/\Gamma(1-s_{2}/2-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)
in
\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s_{2})
, which locate at
s_{2}=2n+2-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu
with
n=0,1,2,\cdots
. Thus, the poles in the second line of (
201
) clash with these zeros if
2n_{1}-n_{2}
happens to be a positive even integer. As a result, among all poles in (
201
), only the following ones make nonzero contributions to the final results:

\begin{cases}s_{2}=-2n_{2},~~~~&(n_{2}=0,1,2,\cdots)\\
s_{2}=2n_{1}-(2n_{2}+1)-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,&(n_{2}=0,1,2,\cdots)\\
s_{2}=2n_{1}-2n_{2}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu.&(n_{2}=n_{1},n_{1}+1,n_{1}+2,\cdots)\end{cases}

(202)

So much for the poles involving
s_{1}=-2n_{1}
. Now let us return to (
200
) and consider the second set of
s_{1}
-poles, namely
s_{1}=-s_{2}-n_{1}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu
. After evaluating the Mellin integrand (
199
) at these poles, we get a factor
x^{s_{2}}
. Since we are considering the region
k_{12}>k_{34}
, namely
x=k_{34}/k_{12}<1
, the factor
x^{s_{2}}
suggests that we should take the right
s_{2}
-poles. There is only one set of right poles from the factor
\Gamma[1/2-s_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu]
in
\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s_{2})
:

\displaystyle s_{2}=\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+n_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu.~~~~(n_{1,2}=0,1,2,\cdots)

(203)

Naively, one may expect that there is another set of right poles coming from the factor
\Gamma(s_{1}/2)=\Gamma[(-s_{2}-n_{1}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)/2]
in
\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s_{1})
, since we are now evaluating
s_{1}
at
s_{1}=-s_{2}-n_{1}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu
. However, this is overcounting since the pole from the
\Gamma(s_{1}/2)
factor has already been included in (
200
). So, we should not include them again here.

In summary, to compute
\mathbf{J}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}(r_{1},x)
we need to pick up the following four sets of poles from the Mellin integrand in (
199
):

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &s_{1}=-2n_{1},s_{2}=-2n_{2},~~~~&&(n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N})\\
&s_{1}=-2n_{1},s_{2}=2n_{1}-(2n_{2}+1)-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,&&(n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N})\\
&s_{1}=-2n_{1},s_{2}=2n_{1}-2n_{2}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,&&(n_{1}\in\mathbb{N};n_{2}-n_{1}\in\mathbb{N})\\
&s_{1}=-s_{2}-n_{1}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}=\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+n_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu.&&(n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N})\end{aligned}\right.

(204)

The contributions from these poles in order are:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{1}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},x)=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{n_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{2}=0}^{\infty}-\mathrm{i}\times 2^{1-2n_{1}-2n_{2}+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\pi^{3}\mathrm{csch}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)^{2}r_{1}^{2n_{1}+2n_{2}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}x^{-2n_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+n_{1},1+n_{2},1+n_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,1+n_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr],

(205)

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},x)=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{n_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{2}=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{3/2+n_{1}+n_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}2^{-2n_{2}}\pi^{2}\coth(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)r_{1}^{1+2n_{2}}x^{-1+2n_{1}-2n_{2}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+n_{1}-n_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-2n_{1}+2n_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+n_{1},\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-n_{1}+n_{2},1+n_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr],

(206)

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},x)=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{n_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{2}=n_{1}}^{\infty}(-1)^{-n_{1}+n_{2}}2^{1-2n_{2}}\pi^{2}e^{-\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\coth(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)r_{1}^{2n_{2}}x^{2n_{1}-2n_{2}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,n_{1}-n_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-2n_{1}+2n_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+n_{1},1-n_{1}+n_{2},1+n_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr],

(207)

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{4}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},x)=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{n_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{2}=0}^{\infty}\mathrm{i}^{2n_{2}-n_{1}}2^{-n_{1}}\pi^{2}e^{-\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\coth(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)r_{1}^{n_{1}}x^{1/2+n_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}1+n_{1}+n_{2},-\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{n_{1}}\,}{2}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{n_{2}}\,}{2}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{n_{2}}\,}{2}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\\
1+n_{2},\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{4}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{n_{2}}\,}{2}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{n_{1}}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{n_{2}}\,}{2}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\end{matrix}\biggr].

(208)

Then we get the full result for the integral
\mathbf{J}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}
as:

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}(r_{1},x)=\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{1}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},x)+\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},x)+\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},x)+\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{4}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},x).

(209)

Computing
\mathbf{J}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)}

For the second integral
\mathbf{J}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)}
in (
142
), we again take the MB representation of its numerator
\mathbf{D}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)}
in (
5
). Then, the integral
\mathbf{J}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)}
becomes:

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)}(r_{1},x)=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{-x}{\mathrm{d}}r\displaystyle\frac{\,{(1-e^{-2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu})(-r)^{1-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{r(r-r_{1})}\mathbf{G}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r/x)\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(1-e^{-2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu})\int_{-\infty}^{-x}{\mathrm{d}}r\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-r)^{1-s_{1}-s_{2}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}x^{s_{1}}}\,}{r(r-r_{1})}\mathbb{G}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s_{1})\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s_{2}),

(210)

where
\mathbb{G}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s_{1})
is the MB representation of
\mathbf{G}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r/x)
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s_{1})=-2^{2s_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\coth(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Gamma\Bigl[s_{1},s_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-2s_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Bigr].

(211)

After finishing the integral over
r
, we get:

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(2)}(r_{1},x)=

\displaystyle\ (1-e^{-2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu})\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}x^{-s_{2}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\Gamma[s_{1}+s_{2}+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu]

\displaystyle\times{}_{2}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathrm{F}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,s_{1}+s_{2}+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+s_{1}+s_{2}+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{x}\biggr]\mathbb{G}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s_{1})\mathbb{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(s_{2}).

(212)

The analysis of poles are very similar to the previous case and here we only list the result. That is, there are two sets of poles contributing to the integral when all momentum ratios taking values from their physical region, together with the condition
k_{12}>k_{34}
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &s_{1}=\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+n_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}=-2n_{2},\\
&s_{1}=\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+n_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}=-\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-n_{1}-n_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu.~~~~(n_{1,2}=0,1,2,\cdots)\end{aligned}\right.

(213)

The contribution from the first set of poles is:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{5}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},x)=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{n_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{2}=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{1/2+n_{1}}2^{{1}/{2}+n_{1}-2n_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{\pi}e^{-\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\coth(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{n_{1}}\,}{2}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{n_{1}}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\\
1+n_{1},1+n_{2},1+n_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\biggr]

\displaystyle\times{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\biggl[\begin{matrix}1,\frac{1}{2}+n_{1}-2n_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}+n_{1}-2n_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\bigg|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{x}\biggr]x^{2n_{2}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu},

(214)

and the contribution from the second set of poles is:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{6}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},x)=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{n_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{2}=0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{i}^{{3}/{2}+3n_{2}+3n_{1}}}2^{-1-n_{1}}{\pi}e^{-\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\coth(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)r_{1}^{n_{1}}x^{1/2+n_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}

\displaystyle\ \times\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-n_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{n_{2}}\,}{2}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{n_{2}}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\\

Simplifying the result

Now we have finished the computation of the three integrals

\displaystyle\{\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{1}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\}\subset\text{nonlocal signal};

(223)

\displaystyle\{\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\ell}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu};\ell=2,3,5\}\subset\text{local signal};

\displaystyle\{\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\ell}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu};\ell=4,6,7,8,9\}\subset\text{background}.

Below, we will simply these 9 series according the above grouping.

Nonlocal signal

The nonlocal signal, which only comes from
\mathbf{\Upsilon}^{\pm\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}_{1}
, can be directly obtained by finishing the double sum in (
D
), and the result is:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{1}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},x)=2\mathrm{i}\pi\ r_{1}^{-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\ \mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(r_{1})\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(r_{1}/x),

(226)

This gives exactly the nonlocal signal which is the starting point of the line dispersion integral:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{NS}}(r_{1},x)=\displaystyle\frac{\,{1-\mathrm{i}\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{2\pi}r_{1}^{1-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}x^{-1/2+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(r_{1})\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(r_{1}/x)+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu).

(227)

Local signal

,
\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\pm\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}

D
), and (
D
). For

The simplification of

factor in (

) and get

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{5}^{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},x)=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{n_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{2}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{3}=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-1)^{1/2+n_{1}+n_{3}}2^{{1}/{2}+n_{1}-2n_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{\pi}}\,}{\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+n_{1}-2n_{2}+n_{3}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}e^{-\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\coth(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)r_{1}^{n_{3}}x^{2n_{2}-n_{3}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\biggl[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+2n_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{n_{1}}\,}{2}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{n_{1}}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\\
